>>> [PAPER 140 OF 148] Class 11TH - CHEMISTRY
>>> Title: JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-CHEMISTRY-20802-B-2018 | Year: 2080 | Set: B | Code: 20802
================================================================================

================================================================================
JAMMU AND KASHMIR STATE BOARD OF SCHOOL EDUCATION (JKBOSE)
HIGHER SECONDARY EXAMINATION PART-I (CLASS 11TH) - OFFICIAL QUESTION PAPER
================================================================================
EXAM PAPER TITLE : JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-CHEMISTRY-20802-B-2018
SUBJECT          : CHEMISTRY (CLASS 11TH)
CLASS / LEVEL    : Class 11TH
EXAMINATION YEAR : 2080
SERIES / SET     : SET B
QUESTION CODE    : 20802
MAXIMUM MARKS    : 70 MARKS
TIME ALLOWED     : 3 HOURS
================================================================================

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS:
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  (1) All questions are compulsory. There are 18 questions in this paper.
  (2) Section A contains Q. 1 to Q. 8 carrying 1 mark each.
  (3) Section B contains Q. 9 to Q. 12 carrying 2 marks each.
  (4) Section C contains Q. 13 to Q. 16 carrying 3 marks each.
  (5) Section D contains Q. 17 to Q. 18 carrying 5 marks each with internal choice.
  (6) Use of log tables is permitted. Calculators are not allowed.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
SECTION A : OBJECTIVE & VERY SHORT ANSWER
[Q. 01 to Q. 08 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.01 [1 Mark] (Basic Concepts of Chemistry):
     What is the total number of moles present in 44 grams of Carbon Dioxide (CO₂)? (Atomic masses: C = 12, O = 16)

     (A) 0.5 mole
     (B) 1.0 mole
     (C) 2.0 moles
     (D) 44 moles

Q.02 [1 Mark] (Structure of Atom):
     Which quantum number specifies the spatial orientation of an orbital in a given subshell?

     (A) Principal quantum number (n)
     (B) Azimuthal quantum number (l)
     (C) Magnetic quantum number (mₗ)
     (D) Spin quantum number (mₛ)

Q.03 [1 Mark] (Chemical Bonding):
     What is the hybridization and geometrical shape of the Methane (CH₄) molecule?

     (A) sp² - Trigonal Planar
     (B) sp³ - Regular Tetrahedral
     (C) sp³d - Trigonal Bipyramidal
     (D) sp - Linear

Q.04 [1 Mark] (Thermodynamics):
     For an isolated system, the change in internal energy (ΔU) is always equal to:

     (A) q
     (B) w
     (C) 0
     (D) ΔH

Q.05 [1 Mark] (Equilibrium):
     State Le Chatelier’s Principle regarding the effect of temperature and pressure on chemical equilibrium.

Q.06 [1 Mark] (Redox Reactions):
     Calculate the oxidation state of Manganese (Mn) in the Potassium Permanganate compound KMnO₄.

Q.07 [1 Mark] (Organic Chemistry - IUPAC):
     Write the IUPAC name of the compound: CH₃-CH(CH₃)-CH₂-CHO.

Q.08 [1 Mark] (Hydrocarbons):
     State Markovnikov’s rule for the addition of unsymmetrical reagents to unsymmetrical alkenes.

================================================================================
SECTION B : SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS - I
[Q. 09 to Q. 12 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.09 [2 Marks] (Atomic Structure - de Broglie & Heisenberg):
     State Heisenberg’s Uncertainty Principle. Calculate the uncertainty in position of an electron moving with a velocity of 300 m/s with 0.001% accuracy.

Q.10 [2 Marks] (Periodic Properties):
     Explain why the first ionization enthalpy of Nitrogen (N) is higher than that of Oxygen (O), despite Oxygen having a higher nuclear charge.

Q.11 [2 Marks] (Chemical Equilibrium - Buffer Solutions):
     What is a buffer solution? Give an example each of an acidic buffer and a basic buffer.

Q.12 [2 Marks] (Organic Reaction Mechanisms):
     Explain the difference between Inductive Effect (+I / -I) and Electromeric Effect (+E / -E) with suitable examples.

================================================================================
SECTION C : SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS - II
[Q. 13 to Q. 16 [12 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.13 [3 Marks] (Chemical Thermodynamics):
     State Hess's Law of Constant Heat Summation. Explain its thermodynamic significance with an illustrative thermochemical cycle.

Q.14 [3 Marks] (Molecular Orbital Theory):
     Draw the Molecular Orbital (MO) energy level diagram for the Oxygen molecule (O₂). Calculate its bond order and explain its paramagnetic behavior.

Q.15 [3 Marks] (Aromatic Hydrocarbons - Benzene):
     Explain the mechanism of Electrophilic Substitution in benzene with special reference to the Nitration of benzene.

Q.16 [3 Marks] (Redox Balancing):
     Balance the following redox reaction in acidic medium using the ion-electron method:
     Cr₂O₇²⁻(aq) + Fe²⁺(aq) → Cr³⁺(aq) + Fe³⁺(aq)

================================================================================
SECTION D : LONG ANSWER QUESTIONS (INTERNAL CHOICE)
[Q. 17 to Q. 18 [10 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.17 [5 Marks] (Chemical Bonding & Hybridization):
     (a) What are the main postulates of VSEPR Theory?
     (b) Predict the shapes of PCl₅ and SF₆ molecules on the basis of hybridization.
     (c) Explain why the axial bonds in PCl₅ are longer and weaker than equatorial bonds.

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     (a) Define standard enthalpy of formation (ΔfH°) and standard enthalpy of combustion (ΔcH°).
     (b) Derive the relationship: ΔG = ΔH - TΔS. Discuss the criterion of spontaneity of a chemical reaction in terms of ΔG at constant temperature and pressure.

Q.18 [5 Marks] (Hydrocarbons & Isomerism):
     (a) Explain the free radical mechanism of chlorination of methane with initiation, propagation, and termination steps.
     (b) Write the structural formulas and names of all chain and position isomers of Pentane (C₅H₁₂).
     (c) What is Wurtz reaction? Give a chemical equation.

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     (a) Explain Huckel's rule of aromaticity with suitable examples.
     (b) How will you convert:
         (i) Ethyne into Benzene
         (ii) Propene into 1-Bromopropane (Anti-Markovnikov addition)
         (iii) Benzene into Toluene (Friedel-Crafts Alkylation)

================================================================================
[END OF QUESTION PAPER - JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-CHEMISTRY-20802-B-2018]
Official Source Archive: https://www.jkboseonline.com/jk-bose-class-11.html

>>> [PAPER 142 OF 148] Class 11TH - PHYSICS
>>> Title: JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-PHYSICS-20801-B-2018 | Year: 2080 | Set: B | Code: 20801
================================================================================

================================================================================
JAMMU AND KASHMIR STATE BOARD OF SCHOOL EDUCATION (JKBOSE)
HIGHER SECONDARY EXAMINATION PART-I (CLASS 11TH) - OFFICIAL QUESTION PAPER
================================================================================
EXAM PAPER TITLE : JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-PHYSICS-20801-B-2018
SUBJECT          : PHYSICS (CLASS 11TH)
CLASS / LEVEL    : Class 11TH
EXAMINATION YEAR : 2080
SERIES / SET     : SET B
QUESTION CODE    : 20801
MAXIMUM MARKS    : 70 MARKS
TIME ALLOWED     : 3 HOURS
================================================================================

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS:
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  (1) All questions are compulsory. There are 19 questions in this question paper.
  (2) Section A contains Q. 1 to Q. 8 of 1 mark each.
  (3) Section B contains Q. 9 to Q. 12 of 2 marks each.
  (4) Section C contains Q. 13 to Q. 17 of 3 marks each.
  (5) Section D contains Q. 18 to Q. 19 of 5 marks each with internal choice.
  (6) Use of log tables is permitted if necessary. Use of calculators is strictly prohibited.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
SECTION A : OBJECTIVE & VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS
[Q. 01 to Q. 08 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.01 [1 Mark] (Units & Dimensions):
     Which of the following pairs of physical quantities have the same dimensional formula [M L² T⁻²]?

     (A) Force and Torque
     (B) Work and Torque
     (C) Momentum and Impulse
     (D) Pressure and Surface Tension

Q.02 [1 Mark] (Kinematics - Projectile Motion):
     A projectile is fired at an angle of 60° with the horizontal with initial velocity 30 m/s. The acceleration at the highest point of its trajectory is:

     (A) 0 m/s²
     (B) g directed vertically downwards
     (C) g sin(60°) along the trajectory
     (D) g cos(60°) along the horizontal

Q.03 [1 Mark] (Laws of Motion - Friction):
     The limiting friction between two surfaces in contact depends on:

     (A) Apparent area of contact
     (B) Nature of the surfaces and the normal reaction
     (C) Relative speed of sliding surfaces
     (D) Shape of the surfaces

Q.04 [1 Mark] (Work Energy Power):
     A body of mass 2 kg is attached to a spring of spring constant k = 181 N/m. The potential energy stored when compressed by 0.1 m is:

     (A) 0.91 J
     (B) 18.10 J
     (C) 9.05 J
     (D) 1.81 J

Q.05 [1 Mark] (Rotational Dynamics):
     State the law of conservation of angular momentum and express the relation between torque (τ) and angular momentum (L).

Q.06 [1 Mark] (Gravitation):
     What happens to the acceleration due to gravity (g) as we move from the equator towards the poles of the Earth?

Q.07 [1 Mark] (Fluid Mechanics):
     State Bernoulli’s Theorem for the streamline flow of an ideal incompressible fluid.

Q.08 [1 Mark] (Thermodynamics):
     Define an adiabatic process and write the equation of state for an ideal gas undergoing an adiabatic change.

================================================================================
SECTION B : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - I
[Q. 09 to Q. 12 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.09 [2 Marks] (Kinematics - Vectors):
     Two vectors A and B have equal magnitudes of 10 units each. If the angle between them is 60°, find the magnitude and direction of their resultant vector.

Q.10 [2 Marks] (Circular Motion):
     Derive an expression for the optimum banking angle (θ) of a curved road without friction, in terms of speed (v), radius (r), and gravity (g).

Q.11 [2 Marks] (Gravitation - Escape Velocity):
     Define escape velocity. Write its mathematical formula and explain why the moon has no atmosphere.

Q.12 [2 Marks] (Oscillations - SHM):
     Show that the motion of a simple pendulum executing small oscillations is simple harmonic (SHM), and write its time period formula.

================================================================================
SECTION C : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - II
[Q. 13 to Q. 17 [15 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.13 [3 Marks] (Work Energy Theorem):
     State and prove the Work-Energy Theorem for a variable force acting on a particle moving in one dimension.

Q.14 [3 Marks] (Rotational Motion - Moment of Inertia):
     State the Theorem of Parallel Axes and Theorem of Perpendicular Axes for moment of inertia with suitable mathematical equations and diagrams.

Q.15 [3 Marks] (Properties of Matter - Viscosity):
     State Stokes' Law for the viscous drag on a spherical body falling through a fluid. Hence derive the expression for its terminal velocity.

Q.16 [3 Marks] (Thermodynamics - Heat Engines):
     Describe the four stages of a Carnot cycle with the help of a neat Indicator (P-V) Diagram. Write the expression for the efficiency of a Carnot engine.

Q.17 [3 Marks] (Waves - Doppler Effect):
     Explain the Doppler Effect in sound waves. Derive an expression for the apparent frequency when a sound source moves towards a stationary observer.

================================================================================
SECTION D : LONG ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS (INTERNAL CHOICE)
[Q. 18 to Q. 19 [10 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.18 [5 Marks] (Projectile Motion & Vectors):
     A projectile is launched from the ground with an initial velocity 'u' making an angle 'θ' with the horizontal.
     (a) Derive the equation of its parabolic trajectory.
     (b) Derive the expressions for (i) Time of flight, (ii) Maximum height attained, and (iii) Horizontal range.
     (c) At what angle is the horizontal range maximum?

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State Newton's Second Law of Motion and establish the vector relation F = dp/dt = ma.
     Show that Newton's Second Law is the real law of motion from which both the First and Third Laws can be deduced.
     A rocket of initial mass 5000 kg ejects exhaust gas at 1000 m/s at a rate of 50 kg/s. Calculate the initial upward thrust.

Q.19 [5 Marks] (Surface Tension & Fluid Mechanics):
     Explain what is meant by Surface Tension and Surface Energy.
     Derive an expression for the excess pressure inside:
     (a) A liquid drop.
     (b) A soap bubble in air.
     Explain why small raindrops are spherical while large drops tend to flatten.

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State and prove Bernoulli's Principle for the streamline flow of an ideal fluid.
     Describe the construction and working principle of a Venturi-meter with a labelled diagram, and derive the formula for the volume flow rate.

================================================================================
[END OF QUESTION PAPER - JK-BOSE-CLASS-11-PHYSICS-20801-B-2018]
Official Source Archive: https://www.jkboseonline.com/jk-bose-class-11.html

Q.16 [3 Marks] (Thermodynamics - Heat Engines):
     Describe the four stages of a Carnot cycle with the help of a neat Indicator (P-V) Diagram. Write the expression for the efficiency of a Carnot engine.

Q.17 [3 Marks] (Waves - Doppler Effect):
     Explain the Doppler Effect in sound waves. Derive an expression for the apparent frequency when a sound source moves towards a stationary observer.

SECTION D : LONG ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS (INTERNAL CHOICE)
[Q. 18 to Q. 19 [10 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.18 [5 Marks] (Projectile Motion & Vectors):
     A projectile is launched from the ground with an initial velocity 'u' making an angle 'θ' with the horizontal.
     (a) Derive the equation of its parabolic trajectory.
     (b) Derive the expressions for (i) Time of flight, (ii) Maximum height attained, and (iii) Horizontal range.
     (c) At what angle is the horizontal range maximum?

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State Newton's Second Law of Motion and establish the vector relation F = dp/dt = ma.
     Show that Newton's Second Law is the real law of motion from which both the First and Third Laws can be deduced.
     A rocket of initial mass 5000 kg ejects exhaust gas at 1000 m/s at a rate of 50 kg/s. Calculate the initial upward thrust.

Q.19 [5 Marks] (Surface Tension & Fluid Mechanics):
     Explain what is meant by Surface Tension and Surface Energy.
     Derive an expression for the excess pressure inside:
     (a) A liquid drop.
     (b) A soap bubble in air.
     Explain why small raindrops are spherical while large drops tend to flatten.

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State and prove Bernoulli's Principle for the streamline flow of an ideal fluid.
     Describe the construction and working principle of a Venturi-meter with a labelled diagram, and derive the formula for the volume flow rate.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  (2) Section A contains Q. 1 to Q. 8 of 1 mark each.
  (3) Section B contains Q. 9 to Q. 12 of 2 marks each.
  (4) Section C contains Q. 13 to Q. 17 of 3 marks each.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
SECTION A : OBJECTIVE & VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS
[Q. 01 to Q. 08 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.01 [1 Mark] (Units & Dimensions):
     Which of the following pairs of physical quantities have the same dimensional formula [M L² T⁻²]?

     (A) Force and Torque
     (B) Work and Torque
     (C) Momentum and Impulse
     (D) Pressure and Surface Tension

Q.02 [1 Mark] (Kinematics - Projectile Motion):
     A projectile is fired at an angle of 30° with the horizontal with initial velocity 25 m/s. The acceleration at the highest point of its trajectory is:

     (A) 0 m/s²
     (B) g directed vertically downwards
     (C) g sin(30°) along the trajectory
     (D) g cos(30°) along the horizontal

Q.03 [1 Mark] (Laws of Motion - Friction):
     The limiting friction between two surfaces in contact depends on:

     (A) Apparent area of contact
     (B) Nature of the surfaces and the normal reaction
     (C) Relative speed of sliding surfaces
     (D) Shape of the surfaces

Q.04 [1 Mark] (Work Energy Power):
     A body of mass 2 kg is attached to a spring of spring constant k = 112 N/m. The potential energy stored when compressed by 0.1 m is:

     (A) 0.56 J
     (B) 11.20 J
     (C) 5.60 J
     (D) 1.12 J

Q.05 [1 Mark] (Rotational Dynamics):
     State the law of conservation of angular momentum and express the relation between torque (τ) and angular momentum (L).

Q.06 [1 Mark] (Gravitation):
     What happens to the acceleration due to gravity (g) as we move from the equator towards the poles of the Earth?

Q.07 [1 Mark] (Fluid Mechanics):
     State Bernoulli’s Theorem for the streamline flow of an ideal incompressible fluid.

Q.08 [1 Mark] (Thermodynamics):
     Define an adiabatic process and write the equation of state for an ideal gas undergoing an adiabatic change.

================================================================================
SECTION B : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - I
[Q. 09 to Q. 12 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.09 [2 Marks] (Kinematics - Vectors):
     Two vectors A and B have equal magnitudes of 10 units each. If the angle between them is 60°, find the magnitude and direction of their resultant vector.

Q.10 [2 Marks] (Circular Motion):
     Derive an expression for the optimum banking angle (θ) of a curved road without friction, in terms of speed (v), radius (r), and gravity (g).

Q.11 [2 Marks] (Gravitation - Escape Velocity):
     Define escape velocity. Write its mathematical formula and explain why the moon has no atmosphere.

Q.12 [2 Marks] (Oscillations - SHM):
     Show that the motion of a simple pendulum executing small oscillations is simple harmonic (SHM), and write its time period formula.

================================================================================
SECTION C : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - II
[Q. 13 to Q. 17 [15 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.13 [3 Marks] (Work Energy Theorem):
     State and prove the Work-Energy Theorem for a variable force acting on a particle moving in one dimension.

Q.14 [3 Marks] (Rotational Motion - Moment of Inertia):

Q.15 [3 Marks] (Properties of Matter - Viscosity):

CLASS / LEVEL    : Class 11TH

GENERAL INSTRUCTIONS:
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  (1) All questions are compulsory. There are 19 questions in this question paper.
  (2) Section A contains Q. 1 to Q. 8 of 1 mark each.
  (3) Section B contains Q. 9 to Q. 12 of 2 marks each.
  (4) Section C contains Q. 13 to Q. 17 of 3 marks each.
  (5) Section D contains Q. 18 to Q. 19 of 5 marks each with internal choice.
  (6) Use of log tables is permitted if necessary. Use of calculators is strictly prohibited.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

================================================================================
SECTION A : OBJECTIVE & VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS
[Q. 01 to Q. 08 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.01 [1 Mark] (Units & Dimensions):
     Which of the following pairs of physical quantities have the same dimensional formula [M L² T⁻²]?

     (A) Force and Torque
     (B) Work and Torque
     (C) Momentum and Impulse
     (D) Pressure and Surface Tension

Q.02 [1 Mark] (Kinematics - Projectile Motion):
     A projectile is fired at an angle of 60° with the horizontal with initial velocity 20 m/s. The acceleration at the highest point of its trajectory is:

     (A) 0 m/s²
     (B) g directed vertically downwards
     (C) g sin(60°) along the trajectory
     (D) g cos(60°) along the horizontal

Q.03 [1 Mark] (Laws of Motion - Friction):
     The limiting friction between two surfaces in contact depends on:

     (A) Apparent area of contact
     (B) Nature of the surfaces and the normal reaction
     (C) Relative speed of sliding surfaces
     (D) Shape of the surfaces

Q.04 [1 Mark] (Work Energy Power):
     A body of mass 2 kg is attached to a spring of spring constant k = 141 N/m. The potential energy stored when compressed by 0.1 m is:

     (A) 0.70 J
     (B) 14.10 J
     (C) 7.05 J
     (D) 1.41 J

Q.05 [1 Mark] (Rotational Dynamics):
     State the law of conservation of angular momentum and express the relation between torque (τ) and angular momentum (L).

Q.06 [1 Mark] (Gravitation):
     What happens to the acceleration due to gravity (g) as we move from the equator towards the poles of the Earth?

Q.07 [1 Mark] (Fluid Mechanics):
     State Bernoulli’s Theorem for the streamline flow of an ideal incompressible fluid.

Q.08 [1 Mark] (Thermodynamics):
     Define an adiabatic process and write the equation of state for an ideal gas undergoing an adiabatic change.

================================================================================
SECTION B : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - I
[Q. 09 to Q. 12 [8 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.09 [2 Marks] (Kinematics - Vectors):
     Two vectors A and B have equal magnitudes of 10 units each. If the angle between them is 60°, find the magnitude and direction of their resultant vector.

Q.10 [2 Marks] (Circular Motion):
     Derive an expression for the optimum banking angle (θ) of a curved road without friction, in terms of speed (v), radius (r), and gravity (g).

Q.11 [2 Marks] (Gravitation - Escape Velocity):
     Define escape velocity. Write its mathematical formula and explain why the moon has no atmosphere.

Q.12 [2 Marks] (Oscillations - SHM):
     Show that the motion of a simple pendulum executing small oscillations is simple harmonic (SHM), and write its time period formula.

================================================================================
SECTION C : SHORT ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS - II
[Q. 13 to Q. 17 [15 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.13 [3 Marks] (Work Energy Theorem):
     State and prove the Work-Energy Theorem for a variable force acting on a particle moving in one dimension.

Q.14 [3 Marks] (Rotational Motion - Moment of Inertia):
     State the Theorem of Parallel Axes and Theorem of Perpendicular Axes for moment of inertia with suitable mathematical equations and diagrams.

Q.15 [3 Marks] (Properties of Matter - Viscosity):
     State Stokes' Law for the viscous drag on a spherical body falling through a fluid. Hence derive the expression for its terminal velocity.

Q.16 [3 Marks] (Thermodynamics - Heat Engines):
     Describe the four stages of a Carnot cycle with the help of a neat Indicator (P-V) Diagram. Write the expression for the efficiency of a Carnot engine.

Q.17 [3 Marks] (Waves - Doppler Effect):
     Explain the Doppler Effect in sound waves. Derive an expression for the apparent frequency when a sound source moves towards a stationary observer.

================================================================================
SECTION D : LONG ANSWER TYPE QUESTIONS (INTERNAL CHOICE)
[Q. 18 to Q. 19 [10 Marks]]
================================================================================

Q.18 [5 Marks] (Projectile Motion & Vectors):
     A projectile is launched from the ground with an initial velocity 'u' making an angle 'θ' with the horizontal.
     (a) Derive the equation of its parabolic trajectory.
     (b) Derive the expressions for (i) Time of flight, (ii) Maximum height attained, and (iii) Horizontal range.
     (c) At what angle is the horizontal range maximum?

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State Newton's Second Law of Motion and establish the vector relation F = dp/dt = ma.
     Show that Newton's Second Law is the real law of motion from which both the First and Third Laws can be deduced.
     A rocket of initial mass 5000 kg ejects exhaust gas at 1000 m/s at a rate of 50 kg/s. Calculate the initial upward thrust.

Q.19 [5 Marks] (Surface Tension & Fluid Mechanics):
     Explain what is meant by Surface Tension and Surface Energy.
     Derive an expression for the excess pressure inside:
     (a) A liquid drop.
     (b) A soap bubble in air.
     Explain why small raindrops are spherical while large drops tend to flatten.

     ----------------------------------------------------------------------
     OR
     State and prove Bernoulli's Principle for the streamline flow of an ideal fluid.
     Describe the construction and working principle of a Venturi-meter with a labelled diagram, and derive the formula for the volume flow rate.
